ENGINEERING AUDIT · MEASURED FOLLOW-UP

Non-search engine audit

Representative VPS benchmarks quantify all promising/speculative follow-ups; legal generation improves, while the small search estimate remains near control noise.

2026-11-01 America/Los_Angeles · Baseline a28150ff16194682b6849491c673f0de0e7c67c6 · Production code changed: No

MEASURED FOLLOW-UP · 2 NOVEMBER UTC / 1 NOVEMBER LOS ANGELES

Representative games, measured candidates, explicit limits

Final legal-move generation improved 3.97–43.76% across six recent VPS snapshots. Whole search measured 0.56% faster, with larger same-binary control variation. The accepted changes remove allocations, copies, repeated hash passes and irrelevant second rays; a reliable 0.5% whole-search gain is not established.

Engine changes: PR #210 · Baseline a28150ff16194682b6849491c673f0de0e7c67c6 · Candidate 94798a64583a6b93a57b70f228c5d18e9f28d9ae. Earlier correctness fixes remain in PR #209. The original first-pass evidence is preserved below.

Coverage: All 51 originally promising/speculative findings are accounted for: 44 follow-up areas, including four API/lifecycle designs without a meaningful speed candidate, plus seven prior bug fixes. The original 22 rejected findings were not remeasured.

Six snapshots from three recently completed VPS games

Snapshots include source-game hashes and the matching live replay-engine hash. Three roots per color; development through late game. Snapshot history/repetition is reset and piece ordering is canonical, so these are representative board workloads rather than exact live traces. Only positions and policy settings/hashes were exported. G3ATK, G3FP and G3PT use verified existing local W512-v3 weights with current-main policy code, not the custom live binary.

There are 576 timed AB pairs plus 144 same-binary controls across screens and confirmations, at fixed depth 2/3. All full signatures match: move, score, depth, nodes, q-nodes, static eval and root lines. Final confirmation uses ten balanced repetitions across six roots × three policies. Intervals resample only three source-game clusters and do not capture all persistent-process/host bias. The adjacent same-binary control differs by 1.54%, exceeding the final effect. No strength, clock or tail-latency claim.

Final legal-generation API

931White59511.73310.755-8.33%11.424–12.22910.500–11.325

Reused output; ten 20 ms batch means per arm from two counterbalanced executable passes, not individual-request quantiles. All 91 count/ordered-checksum API cases and 56 allocation cases agree. Opening reused-output allocations 97→74; late 1,400-ply root 261→53, cumulative requested bytes 11,504→1,344. Requested bytes are not RSS. Borrowed snapshots improve about 71–78% and bulk loading 92–98.5% in isolated APIs, outside the search timer.

How to read the remaining experiments

Geometry tables, mailbox/line occupancy, packing, configuration, maintained-count, binary-write and checkpoint figures below are scoped kernels. Where listed, their construction, mutation, decoding, full route handling or cache costs are excluded. A fast kernel does not establish a search gain. Fixed arrays, naïve overlays, simple blocker boxing, exported dispatch and small-list unstable sort did not qualify. Final code uses stable sort below 64 targets, skips unions smaller than two and uses a bitmap only for large unions.

Public Rust API: Free Eagle paths become MovePath with borrowed slices; JSON and arbitrary-length overflow stay supported. The long-route fallback has an extra box. Generated paths avoid heap storage; the type is Clone rather than Copy. The rejected exported dispatch also had a source-level legacy snapshot compatibility issue.

Promising / proven

Limits: Cumulative requested bytes and alloc/realloc call counts, not peak/live memory; warm single-thread global allocator census, no latency measured under counting allocator. Aggregate bundle attribution.

M-adaptive-bitmap-union
Use168-byte square bitmap for large/duplicate-heavy final target unions		Selected: clear work reduction or validation

Selected refined policy: skip<2, stable sort<64, bitmap>=64

Empty Rook70targets595.8->363.1ns (39.1%faster), directly exposing large-union policy. Trial used unstable sort below64: sparse Rook50targets472.1->584.9ns (+23.9%) and Pawn0/1targets+5–16%; reject that small-list unstable-sort variant. Legal generation all6VPS ordered checksums match.

Limits: Whole Tengu/Hook results combine ray streaming+bitmap and cannot be attributed to bitmap alone. Final lean source differs from timed trial by retaining stable small-list sorting and skipping already ordered singleton/empty lists. Parent whole-search bitmap ablation decides inclusion. Initial movement API trial used the rejected unstable small-list path. Final legalAPI and whole-search measurements use the refined stable-sort policy.

Complete case metrics and evidence

Bulk-load validated snapshots and hash once		Selected: clear work reduction or validation

implemented; asymptotically reduces work

Validate BoardPosition once, place directly into a new Board, and restore position once; removes full-board rehash/repetition reseed for every piece.

Limits: Snapshot loading only. State is new, so restore cache-invalidating fixes in PR209 are not required for this optimization. Seeded history/repetition is snapshot semantics.

S09
Avoid Free Eagle list regeneration during trusted application		Selected: clear work reduction or validation

Selected: avoid trusted Free Eagle list regeneration

Generated Standard Free Eagle ranges skip full route-list regeneration only on trusted unrecorded application. Ordinary FE ranges are NoJump; short matching multi-leg prefixes have empty intermediate squares. Checked destination-only route normalization and explicit paths unchanged.

Limits: Kernel old arm emulates existing normalization via public single-piece generation, then trusted apply, so includes public generation-wrapper overhead. Do not equate 98% FE fixture saving to whole-search speedup. Exact legacy first-match normalization tested on every generated Standard FE move in 128 seeded sparse/dense boards, both colors; also 96 corner/center blocker fixtures, board/hash/draw/repetition/undo parity. State bundle72searchpairs ratio0.993279; samebinary controls are larger than this effect, so no independent searchgain asserted.

write-only prototype; possible

Prototype emits 7-byte header +8 bytes per piece preserving type, origin, coordinates, color, promoted flag. Compared serialization with TSFEN and measured opening byte sizes.

Limits: No decoder/versioning/endianness contract/malformed-input validation or end-to-end I/O/training bottleneck measured. Existing text interchange unchanged. Write-only kernel cannot justify changing persisted public formats.

C03
Use replay checkpoints for long interactive sessions		Unintegrated kernel / design; no qualifying search proof

replay kernel prototype; possible

Repeated 64/512-ply two-King reversible sequences compare replay from original state with clone of a state checkpoint plus last32 plies; hashes, repetition_count and history length agree.

Limits: GameState clone preserves repetition/history here, unlike BoardPosition reload. This is not an integrated DebugTool checkpoint cache, branch invalidation/memory budget or GUI latency measurement. Actual exotic-route replay parity and persisted trunk metadata remain needed. Keeping many full GameState checkpoints naively duplicates history prefixes; retention budget or shared immutable history needs design. Prototype stores only one checkpoint per length.

Dubious / rejected · 8 findings
AreaDispositionResult, metrics and limits
M-reuse-existing-direct-probes
Reuse specialized target probes in generic exported multileg reach API		Rejected tested prototype

Dubious / rejected for naive exported dispatch

Specialized reach saves huge query fanout (emptyTengu1015.02->0.900us per42queries), but ordinaryPawn42queries0.223->0.282us (+26.5%),Rook0.332->0.384us (+15.5%). Board attack dispatcher already uses these specialized probes, limiting workflow gain. Independent review found legacy OldKite-is_promoted classifier mismatch with MovementConfig.

Limits: Naive CannonSoldier dispatch also recurses via its existing fallback and was removed before final validation. Future capability-driven dispatch could avoid common-case cost, but no such implementation measured. Reject trial Piece change even though selected proper-promotion fixtures passed. Independent source review also found accepted legacy OldKite+ without baseorigin would be incorrectly classified as Tengu; this dispatch is absent from finalcode.

R19
Allocation-free borrowed board overlays		Rejected tested prototype

measured naive iterator prototype rejected; broader idea remains possible

Compared VirtualBoard::get_pieces_by_color Vec materialization followed by attack_utils candidate-count filtering with borrowed base-army iteration, moved/promoted overlay and removed-victim predicates, then the same candidate-count filtering. Query changes through all1296 squares and is blackboxed. On opening+6VPSsnapshots, both colors, candidate counts agree at100sampletargets percase. The borrowed pipeline regresses13/14 median color-position cases; 6VPS-only subset regresses11/12. Do not ship this prototype.

Limits: Kernel-only and different return shapes: no owned Vec is needed by the caller in the borrowed arm. It measures candidate prefilter counts, not full attack reachability or early exits; no production BoardLike rewrite, ancestor composition or heavy exotic-capture overlay coverage. One ordinary generated move is overlaid per position. This demonstrates that extra per-attacker filtering can offset removed bulk Vec clones; it does not reject every optimized visitor/overlay design. Prior whole-search CPU ratio1.01508 interval0.98089..1.06270 also did not establish a gain.

S17
Consider a maintained draw-material class count		Rejected tested prototype

Reject maintained-count state for now

Borrowed all-draw-class scan tested on 804 Kings versus precomputed zero nonqualifying-count query. Opening borrowed predicate short-circuits in ~1ns here.

Limits: Count prototype ignores update/invalidation cost, extra board state and editor paths; fixture is worst case for a scan and is not a representative material distribution. No src/search.rs draw-material query calls; no search-time benefit established. Borrowed ordinary draw checks already take about1ns here; per-mutator bookkeeping was not measured and a query-only win does not establish positive whole-workflow time.

Reproduction and validation

Intel Core i7-1255U Linux x86_64; Rust 1.92.0 / LLVM 21.1.3; portable release, ThinLTO, one codegen unit, CPU 2. Compiler/test work paused for recorded timing; lightweight metadata/report preparation continued, and unrelated host activity was not isolated. Baseline/candidate executables, source/input hashes, individual pairs, ranges and rejected patches are retained.

Candidate checks: 579 Rust tests passed, four existing ignored; Clippy passed; 227 deployment Python tests passed. Ruff format and repository scope pass; full Ruff reports the same 30 errors in unrelated untracked analysis/backup files. Corpus preparation verifies real local weights and rejects a mismatched material hash. No VPS deployment or tournament changes.

Reproduction and evidence guide · All search statistics · Final API samples/ranges · Build/input/source manifest · Complete follow-up JSON

Area inventory

10 areas · 73 findings

Rules, legal/pseudo-legal generation, board and move representations, state application/simulation, hashes, draw/lifecycle machinery and agent-facing interchange. Search algorithms, move ordering/pruning, evaluation formulas, neural inference and training were excluded. Cache lifecycle at state boundaries was included.

  1. 01

    Board storage

    Square lookup, per-color dense lists, slot indices, swap removal, royal/global masks, heap versus inline arrays, SoA, packed pieces.

  2. 02

    Move and route storage

    Standard/two-step/Free Eagle shape, full victim identity, inline paths, Copy specialization, u64 packing, wire compatibility.

  3. 03

    Geometry and sliders

    Square arithmetic, next-square and ray tables, delta geometry, mailbox sentinels, line occupancy, bitscans, magic/PEXT scaling.

  4. 04

    Movement execution

    Simple/range/jump/conditional patterns, origin-sensitive promotion forms, perpendicular second legs, overlays, count/any/stream APIs.

  5. 05

    Target unions and filters

    Dedup sort/bitmap choices, tiny-list overhead, ordering, raw multiplicity, capture-mode specialization, route coalescing.

  6. 06

    Attack queries

    Directed probes, cached metadata, local/global attacker masks, virtual attacker scans, eager attack caches and independent rule checks.

  7. 07

    State transitions

    Checked/trusted application, transactionality, path captures including friendly sweeps, make/unmake, hash parity and promotion.

  8. 08

    Game lifecycle

    Royal capture, progress clock, repeated positions, prefix caches, insufficient material, reset/restore/edit invariants.

  9. 09

    Interchange and consumers

    BoardPosition/JSON, TSFEN/TM1, legacy records, UCI admission, session snapshots, timeline cloning and replay.

  10. 10

    Rust and build technique

    Enum/niche/layout facts, Copy versus Clone, fixed/static descriptors, OnceLock, allocation reuse, LTO/native/unsafe tradeoffs.

Recommended sequence

  1. Fix reproduced state/interchange issues in focused PRs: Free Eagle final history leg, promotion admission, restore cache invalidation, legacy coordinate validation and documented TM1 routes.
  2. Take low-risk allocation wins next: borrowed terminal/snapshot APIs, bulk validated snapshot construction, residual fixed direction arrays and target-only dead path storage.
  3. Integrate one generation change at a time: perpendicular second rays first, then adaptive target bitmap/streamed multi-leg output. Preserve route/progress effects and ordered move results.
  4. Trial Copy inline Move before pervasive bit packing; benchmark complete generation/apply/clone workloads with realistic games. Packed Piece full decoding already lost the local comparison.
  5. Treat line occupancy, compiled movement signatures, selective caches and alternate layouts as experiments requiring update-cost and integrated parity measurements.

Evidence and limits

12,461 sampled generated moves passed validated/trusted application, simulation, full-hash and undo checks. Movement comparisons passed 401,186 directed reach, 144,048 candidate-filter and 134,784 specialized-probe checks. Turn prototype passed 1,152 ordered target-list cases; line prototype passed 31,104 rook counts; representation checks passed 387 move roundtrips, 18,180 piece roundtrips, 4,096 square reads and 16 draw/snapshot cases. These checks share movement configurations and are not an independent historical-rules oracle. No long-game randomized differential, sanitizer/Miri campaign, fuzz campaign, integrated performance confirmation or playing-strength experiment was run. The generated samples are moves, not independent games. Undo checks compare square contents, turn, counter, hash and current repetition count; they do not prove restoration of piece-list order or byte-identical history. Evaluator caches were unbound during the broad state sample.

Method and environment

First-pass screen on Intel i7-1255U Linux x86_64, rustc 1.92.0 / LLVM 21.1.3, release ThinLTO and one codegen unit, default portable CPU target, taskset CPU 2. Baseline and candidate helpers run in the same executable with black_box; most timing groups alternate arm order. Repetitions are batch means, not individual latency quantiles. CPU frequency/background load were not controlled; these are pilot measurements with ranges, not formal confidence intervals. Packing, prebuilt geometry construction, and mutation overhead are excluded where specified. Baseline production revision is the SHA above; candidate identity is the named example plus its recorded content hash.

Promising / proven

20 findings
C01 Priority 1 Agent-facing APIsoptimization

Use allocation census to target remaining temporary storage

Evidence Executed allocation census
Finding, limits and references

Warmed opening generation returns 280 moves with 105 allocation/reallocation calls and 34120 total requested bytes. Reusing its final Vec reduces this to 97 calls and 1480 requested bytes: intermediate storage remains. Captures-only returns 80 moves with 44 calls. This identifies a real allocation budget for streamed targets and inline paths.

Limits

One opening fixture; requested allocation bytes are cumulative, not live/peak memory. Global allocator instrumentation was used for counts, not latency claims. The genuine quiet make/unmake and quiet delta construction each allocate zero; do not optimize imaginary per-quiet capture allocations.

  • examples/audit_core_probe.rs
  • src/game_state.rs:1154
Measured follow-up

Promising / proven · Selected: clear work reduction or validation

M-adaptive-bitmap-union Priority 1 Move generation / attacksoptimization

Use168-byte square bitmap for large/duplicate-heavy final target unions

Evidence Measured local kernel + parity
Finding, limits and references

Dedup-only paired median speedups3.23–8.78x on tested duplicate-rich Lion/Tengu/HookMover/FE lists. Empty Rook2.48x; sparse Rook2.75x. Exact final vectors agree. Empty Hook raw2590 ->1295unique: stable-sort median35.88us vs bitmap4.04us. Empty Tengu raw1294 ->647unique:16.34us vs1.90us.

Limits

Pawn~2.3xslower and dense3targetRook~1.6xslower. Need adaptive policy and integrated generator timing. Bitmap target union cannot replace complete route identity.

R03 Priority 1 Representation / Rustoptimization

Inline Free Eagle routes in Move

Evidence Measured local kernel + parity
Finding, limits and references

One Vec-containing MoveData variant inflates every standard and two-step move and each Free Eagle route clone allocates. Current generator routes fit five Positions including origin. An inline enum with [Position;5] and u8 length preserves full route identity, return captures and path order. The final Copy inline representation is 17 B versus 32 B current Move. Opening clone control with Clone only was 3.70x slower, while identical Copy storage cloned much faster. Smaller layout alone is insufficient; Copy enables bulk copying.

Limits

This is a storage/clone prototype, not integrated generator or execution performance. Public MoveData serde currently accepts arbitrary Vec lengths; production conversion needs validation or a large-route fallback and stable serialization. Whole move generation, copying and agent throughput still need measurement. A generic SmallVec with heap fallback may itself keep a pointer-sized union and expand the Move; validate actual size rather than substituting it automatically.

S01 Priority 1 State / interchangeoptimization

Bulk-load validated snapshots and hash once

Evidence Measured core API + state parity
Finding, limits and references

Bulk snapshot loading eliminates repeated full-board hashing. BoardPosition::to_state at src/board_position.rs:85-96 calls GameState::place_piece for every piece; place_piece at src/game_state.rs:600-605 recomputes the entire current board hash and reseeds repetition each time. This is quadratic in snapshot size. The equivalent prototype validates once, places into Board directly, then calls restore_position once. The full 804-piece opening matched every square and final hash. Revised 8 rounds x300 loads with 30 warmups/method and alternating order measured median batch means 489,923.59 ns/current versus 6,424.86 ns/bulk per load, a 76.25x ratio or 98.69% reduction. Current round means ranged 478,988.82-525,111.31 ns; bulk ranged 6,247.30-13,028.44 ns. These are repeated-batch means, not individual-request tail latencies or independent statistical confidence intervals. This is snapshot loading only, not whole-game or search throughput. Implementation is small; retain validation and one canonical hash/repetition reset.

S02 Priority 1 State / interchangecorrectness

Free Eagle history omits the final leg

Evidence Reproduced correctness / contract issue
Finding, limits and references

FreeEagle generated moves omit the final leg in in-engine history. src/game_state.rs:2048-2058 intentionally skips the final entry when path[i]==mv.to, and the following condition also skips it because path.last()==mv.to. Generated one-leg path history length is 0; generated two-, three-, four-leg path histories have lengths 1, 2, 3. This is reproducible for actual generated moves, with no malformed input. Any consumer of GameState::get_move_history gets incomplete history; evaluate() currently derives default ply from history length, while external GameHistory records are independent. Fix final-leg recording and decide explicitly whether this API counts logical turns or physical legs (TwoStep currently records both legs). Avoid silently changing both semantics in one patch.

S03 Priority 1 State / interchangecorrectness

Check promotion flags at external move admission

Evidence Reproduced correctness / contract issue
Finding, limits and references

Promotion validation is incomplete at public-input boundaries. make_move documents an assumption that the caller validated, yet invokes apply_move(validate=true), and UCI position-moves invokes it directly as the legality gate. execute_single_move at src/game_state.rs:2167-2174 trusts mv.promoted. Probes demonstrate: Pawn 8,8->8,9+ promotes outside the zone even though not generated; Pawn 8,34->8,35 without + declines compulsory promotion even though not generated; an already Pawn-promoted GoldGeneral promotes again to Rook, retains base Pawn and makes BoardPosition::validate reject the resulting state; a King move carrying + leaves the King unpromoted but resets progress clock 99->0. These are malformed-external-move cases; normal generated moves passed parity checks. Centralize promotion eligibility, compulsory promotion and actual promotion outcome validation in checked application. Keep the trusted generated-move fast contract separate so checking does not slow every agent-generated move. Prioritize external UCI/import correctness rather than calling generated movement broken.

S09 Priority 1 State / interchangeoptimization

Avoid Free Eagle list regeneration during trusted application

R07 Priority 2 Representation / Rustoptimization

Position as a single linear u16

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Current file:u8,rank:u8 is already2 bytes, so replacing with u16 does not compact Position. It makes to_index trivial but makes file/rank/offset conversion costlier, and can change derived ordering from file-major to rank-major.

Limits

Treat as arithmetic tradeoff only; no size win. Piece ordering and sort-dedup order are behavioral constraints. Division by constant36 is normally compiler-strength-reduced; inspect generated code before assuming hardware division. A NonZeroU16 index could shrink Option<Position> from3 to2 bytes through a niche, but plain Position remains2 bytes and the obvious current single-royal cache saving is only2 bytes total.

  • src/position.rs:49
  • src/position.rs:54
  • src/position.rs:71
Measured follow-up

Speculative / possible · Unintegrated kernel / design; no qualifying search proof

R08 Priority 2 Representation / Rustoptimization

Fixed-size heap square arrays

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

squares is always1296 entries and piece_slots always1296; Box<[T;1296]> can express fixed shape and remove Vec length/capacity words. Fixed-length arrays may expose more bounds facts to the optimizer. Source-derived header savings are16 bytes for squares Vec→fixed Box and8 bytes for dynamic-slice piece_slots→fixed Box,24 bytes per Board before padding.

Limits

Only tens of header bytes are saved; heap payload dominates. Inline Board arrays enlarge stack frames and clones, especially recursive callers. No blanket claim that Box is faster; current owned Vec clone is already contiguous memcpy-compatible.

  • src/board.rs:9
  • src/board.rs:29
  • src/board.rs:281
Measured follow-up

Dubious / rejected · Rejected tested prototype

R09 Priority 2 Representation / Rustoptimization

Piece array-of-structs versus struct-of-arrays

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Armies are already separated by color. Position-only or type-only scans could read less data with SoA, while complete Piece queries/placements would need multiple arrays and synchronization. Removing redundant per-army color is another small specialization.

Limits

Most movement probes consume both type/config and position, so AoS locality is useful. Type count303 makes large per-type side arrays unattractive. Benchmark real scans and swaps rather than assuming SoA wins.

  • src/piece.rs:998

R18 Priority 2 State / allocationoptimization

Inline common capture deltas with overflow fallback

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Most ordinary deltas have zero or one captured Piece yet allocate a Vec on capture. SmallVec-style inline capacity1-4 could avoid common allocations. moved_pieces currently returns a Vec for at most one moved Piece; an Option iterator or slice-shaped accessor is simpler.

Limits

Capturing-range rays can remove many pieces, so fixed capacity4 without spill is wrong. Larger inline buffers increase delta size/move costs. affected_pieces_for_attack_detection appears unused outside its own helpers/tests; avoid optimizing dead infrastructure.

S10 Priority 2 State / interchangecorrectness

Make raw board edits refresh state invariants

Finding, limits and references

Replace raw get_board_mut with an edit operation/guard that commits all state invariants. src/game_state.rs:544 clears eval_inc/nnue but returns &mut Board without updating hash/repetition after caller mutations. Probe moves a Pawn using get_board_mut and confirms hash!=zobrist::compute, while repetition_count still reports 1 for the obsolete hash. Existing production use is limited to bin/test_free_eagle; evaluation tests also use it. S12 Priority 2 State / interchangeoptimization

Share a canonical move-effects description

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Share a single move-effects/delta description across application, simulation, capture classification and undo. Today apply_move/execute_single_move, move_to_delta, FreeEagle loops and capture predicates separately encode the same exotic effects. A validated effect record can make parity easier and update hash incrementally in non-search make_move instead of full-board recompute. Avoid replacing cheap make/unmake with per-move Board clones. S16 Priority 2 State / interchangeoptimization

Consider bounded reachable-form Zobrist tables

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Precomputed Zobrist keys versus arithmetic splitmix. src/zobrist.rs deterministically hashes a packed identity on demand. A lookup can replace multiplications, but a full 303-type x2-color x2-promoted x1296-square table is about 12.6 MB before promotion-origin variants. Sparse tables for reachable forms, or a smaller square key combined with piece-form key, deserve a microbenchmark plus representative make/unmake benchmark. Current packing fields are disjoint and includes promotion origin specifically because Whales can move differently. No speed claim and no justification for losing origin metadata or changing identity semantics merely to fit a small chess table.

Limits

No separate limitation recorded.

Maintain terminal material-class counts only if profiling justifies it. Borrowing army slices removes immediate allocations; maintaining a count of pieces outside the current draw class could turn the scan constant-time and fold into existing board mutation bookkeeping. Additional state/invalidation paths and arbitrary editor snapshots add risk; likely low priority compared with borrowed scans that usually short-circuit early.

S18 Priority 2 State / interchangeoptimization

Separate optional binary snapshot storage from text interchange

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Snapshot packing/binary cache is possible for large replay/training datasets. Existing TSFEN sorts and converts piece enums through serde for each token, and snapshot JSON is verbose. Separate text interchange (readable/stable) from optional compact internal snapshot storage. YaneuraOu has PackedSfen; its exact 32-byte representation does not generalize to 804 pieces/303 types/1296 squares. No data-size or I/O bottleneck demonstrated here.

S20 Priority 2 State / interchangeAPI design

Make checked move admission transactional

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Public checked move application should be transactional by construction: validate the full route/promotion/effects first, then mutate. Existing checked FreeEagle already does this and has an explicit regression test; checked TwoStep validates both legs before moving. The trusted make_for_search path can partially mutate if deliberately given a malformed multi-leg route with friendly second destination, but its documented contract requires generated legal moves. Do not claim this as a generated-move bug; narrow visibility or a validated-move token could make the contract harder to misuse.

Limits

Use replay checkpoints for long interactive sessions

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

apply_live_move applies a move then rebuilds the complete position from its initial/setup root. Navigation also replays from the root. Periodic validated checkpoints or reversible session state could reduce repeated work on long records.

Limits

No replay timing collected. Current rebuild is deliberately a reliability mechanism for exotic routes. Checkpoints must preserve turn, draw clock, repetition history, branch structure and any caches; this is not a reason to weaken replay validation.

Finding, limits and references

Separate editor side-setting from null-move side-setting. set_current_turn updates hash but not repetition history; calling it on a newly seeded state makes repetition_count report 0. Search null-move callers intentionally push/pop repetition separately, and snapshot loaders reset afterward. Introduce a checked/editor reset operation or document this low-level contract; automatically resetting in the current method would break valid callers. R13 Priority 2 Geometry / occupancyoptimization

Full conventional magic/PEXT lookup per giant ray

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Chess magic tables enumerate relevant occupancy subsets. A36x36 rook has up to70 ray squares, up to68 relevant non-edge blockers, so a direct occupancy-subset table becomes infeasible. Even a single36-square line can have34 relevant bits, over17billion subsets.

Limits

Chunked byte tables, segmented bitboards, line bit scans or hyperbola-style arithmetic remain possible; reject the direct one-table transplant, not all hardware bit extraction.

R14 Priority 2 Geometry / occupancyoptimization

Bitboard per piece type and color

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

606 full21-word bitboards require101808 bytes before existing board data. Most types are sparse, so copying chess's all-type masks wholesale is expensive compared with compact piece lists. Existing occupied/global-attacker masks and sparse royal index cover useful aggregates.

Limits

A few capability groups or active-word masks may help; YaneuraOu explicitly combines movement-equivalent groups to reduce table fetches. Maintaining eight distant-direction indices already screened worse historically.

R05 Priority 2 Representation / Rustoptimization

Pack full Piece identity into u32

Evidence Measured local kernel + parity
Finding, limits and references

303 current types use9 bits; optional original base uses9 with sentinel511; color1, promoted1, and square11 total31 bits. A spare high-bit sentinel can represent empty without a second tag. All promotion provenance must remain: Whale movement depends on original ReverseChariot and RainDragon on promoted status. Baseline Option<Piece> is already8 bytes, so the1296-square mirror would shrink10368→5184 bytes. Current full-decode prototype was slower: paired median ratio1.34608, range1.18142..1.46146 over nine batches. The tested full-decoding implementation was slower: 4096 mixed square probes median 6408.323 ns current versus 9274.115 ns packed. Reject this decoder; metadata-only compact storage remains a separate possible design.

Limits

Prototype safely decodes through ALL_PIECE_TYPES rather than transmuting enum values. It is an independent read-only square store; mutator costs and complete generation are unmeasured. Storing only metadata and using the queried Position may remove index decoding cost. Verdict applies to this full-decode prototype, not every packed layout; memory size alone did not predict speed.

  • src/piece.rs:998
  • src/board.rs:9
  • src/movement/config.rs:104
  • src/zobrist.rs:30
R23 Priority 2 Representation / Rustoptimization

Rust inlining, unchecked indexing, target-cpu and SIMD

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Release already uses thin LTO and one codegen unit; BoardLike uses generics and can monomorphize, so there is no blanket dynamic-dispatch problem. Position construction/serde is checked, but compiler cannot always infer private-field range. Profile-selected #[inline] or layout tuning is possible; blanket inline(always), unsafe get_unchecked, repr(packed) and SIMD without workload evidence are poor first changes. Actual Piece size8 equals the sum of its current field sizes, so repr(packed) would not even save padding in this structure.

Limits

repr(packed) can introduce unaligned access and troublesome references. target-cpu=native reduces binary portability; benefit is workload dependent. Existing historical PGO/native results should inform any followup.

  • Cargo.toml:26
  • src/board.rs:43
  • src/position.rs:7
  • src/movement/generator.rs:12
R24 Priority 2 Representation / Rustoptimization

Do not transplant endpoint-only Move sentinels or strip provenance

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Stockfish/YaneuraOu reserve from==to encodings for special/null moves because ordinary moves do not return to origin. This variant has real capture-and-return routes, and distinct intermediate/path routes with identical endpoints can remove different victims. A compact move must preserve kind, intermediate/full path, promotion and origin; Piece must retain movement-relevant promotion provenance.

Limits

Prototype u64 does preserve this information for current generated routes. Simplifying equality/hash to endpoints is a bug, not an optimization.

  • src/game_state.rs:2508
  • src/movement/config.rs:104

R20 Priority 2 State / allocationoptimization

Avoid giant Zobrist lookup tables

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Current keys use a cheap deterministic SplitMix64 of packed identity and update incrementally. A naive table covering303 types x304 base choices x2colors x2promoted x1296squares is477513216 entries,3.82GB decimal; allowing all base/origin combinations is mostly unnecessary, and reachable-origin subset is much smaller.

Limits

A generated table for only reachable identities could still be benchmarked, but eliminating a few integer multiplies may lose to cache misses. Full keys distinguish promotion origin intentionally; discarding it is a correctness regression.

  • src/zobrist.rs:30
  • src/zobrist.rs:67
R21 Priority 2 State / allocationoptimization

Preserve existing O(1) piece-list removal and royal caches

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Board already has u16 square-to-slot index, swap_remove captures, per-color occupied masks, special-global-attacker masks, royal count/sparse positions/single-royal cache and borrowed army access. Replacing these with HashMaps, scanning every square, or repeatedly locating kings would regress established design.

Limits

swap_remove changes piece iteration order after captures deliberately; stable-order replacements may change move ordering or cost. Current tests cover index/cache consistency.

S23 Priority 2 State / interchangeoptimization

Preserve this variant's royal-capture legality semantics

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Do not import chess pin/self-check legality or single-king loss assumptions. This engine intentionally generates pseudo-legal moves and loses when all King/CrownPrince royals are gone; same-side royals can coexist. is_legal_move_assuming_reachable's optional self-check path is not enabled by normal generation. A chess-style blanket king-safety filter would change game rules and search move choice, outside optimization scope.

Limits

No separate limitation recorded.

S24 Priority 2 State / interchangeoptimization

Do not import ordinary-shogi hand/drop/repetition rules

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Do not import standard-shogi drops, nifu, pawn-drop mate, perpetual-check adjudication, or YaneuraOu's fourfold repetition as automatic fixes. No captures-to-hand/drop system exists here; current fivefold/progress/custom insufficient-material rules are explicit. Historical variant rule verification would be a separate task.

S25 Priority 2 State / interchangeoptimization

Use spill-capable capture and move buffers

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Do not cap capture logs at one/two victims or board/move capacity at chess MAX_MOVES=256. Sweeping and multi-leg moves, 1296 squares and hundreds of pieces require spill-capable storage and data-driven capacity checks. Do not infer clone cost from Board's 824-byte header; most storage is heap owned.

Limits

No separate limitation recorded.

S26 Priority 2 State / interchangeoptimization

Preserve collision-safe repetition prefix indexing

Evidence Source + existing regression tests
Finding, limits and references

Do not replace existing repeated-key prefix indexing with a HashMap automatically. RepetitionPrefix uses sorted counts plus a low-bit presence filter, with collision-safe full-key binary search and suffix scanning; it already addresses long histories without per-move map updates. Existing test suite covers shortening prefix, collisions and independent clones. Profiling needed for alternative structures. Truncating repetition history on any progress counter reset imports assumptions that require proof for all exotic movement/promotion states.

Limits

No separate limitation recorded.

Evidence Source / feasibility
Finding, limits and references

Full board clone per simulated move is rejected as default direction. Existing virtual boards/make-unmake already avoid copying 1296 squares plus lists and indices. Their local allocation issues can be targeted without reverting to copy/make.

Limits

Measurement appendix

73 primary cases

Times are nanoseconds per defined batch. Each arm is the median of repetition-level batch means. Candidate / baseline ratios below 1 mean lower candidate time. The range is the observed paired-repetition minimum–maximum.

Reading these numbers

Observed paired-repetition range only; no confidence interval, individual-operation p95/p99 or workload-level latency distribution is inferred. Kernels, workloads and batch sizes differ. No combined engine speedup is computed. Expand each case for arm definitions, workload and excluded costs.

  • The final Cargo-built turn output supersedes the initial standalone-rustc turn timing; both raw files are archived.
  • The initial representation run and followup are both archived. Use representation-copy.txt for the Copy result; Clone-only control remains in that same final binary.
  • core.jsonl combines the original line timing run with a later corrected, explicit sample-versus-quiet allocation census; these are separate measurements.
  • Full Rust and Python checks passing does not mean the reproduced bugs are fixed. Bug probes record present behavior; only parity invariants fail the executable on disagreement.

Engine ideas and primary sources

  • Stockfish: moves and generation

    Target masks, caller-provided output, generation specialization and optional SIMD emission inspire narrow generation changes. Its chess pin/check and fixed capacities do not transfer directly.

  • Stockfish: compact Move

    The chess move uses 16 bits. Taikyoku needs 11 bits per square and route identity; our safe complete-move prototype uses 64 bits.

  • Stockfish: state and attacks

    Incremental state, reversible per-move information and target-oriented attack queries are useful patterns. This repo already implements much of that structure.

  • Stockfish: architecture-specific slider techniques

    Contemporary source contains multiple slider techniques rather than one universal magic implementation. Per-line arithmetic is the more plausible adaptation to 36x36.

  • YaneuraOu: generation modes

    Target bitboards, caller output and specialized generation modes are applicable ideas. Modes that omit promotion choices cannot replace an all-legal-moves API.

  • YaneuraOu: Move16 and Move32

    Separate compact internal moves from richer execution metadata. Its no-op/null sentinels are unsafe to copy when real capture-and-return moves exist.

  • YaneuraOu: state and board groups

    Caller-owned StateInfo, distinct checked/trusted move contracts, selected movement groups and packed interchange offer design ideas without importing standard-shogi rules.

  • YaneuraOu: multiword bitboards

    Its two-word 81-square representation illustrates adapting bitboard width to a board. Our board requires 21 u64 words; line-local masks avoid scanning all 21 for simple rays.

  • Fairy-Stockfish: generalized riders and leapers

    Shared rider/leaper classes and variant masks suggest compiling by movement signature. Dense tables still become large for 303 types and 1296 squares.

  • Arasan programmer guide

    Its square array plus bitboards, 64-bit move records and saved reversible state show that hybrid layouts and richer compact moves are viable alternatives to a single representation.

  • Rust type-layout reference

    Actual sizes were measured with rustc 1.92.0 rather than assumed. Rust layout is not a stable serialization format; packed/niche representations need explicit safe conversion.

Offline report · report.json · full measurement data · Back to inventory

Kernel measurements support follow-up experiments. They establish no integrated engine or playing-strength gain.
